# Spec and test gaps: MCI mailbox

Each finding below gives the gap, the evidence for it as exact quotes at their file and line, and how it was verified: an agent searched the repository to refute the finding, and the finding was kept only if that failed. ## 1. Spec too ambiguous to test

### 1.1 Post-MCI-reset SRAM_OWNER encoding: Root (2) or lock holder (1)?

`f_7dbfd12edb46` · found during verification · severity high

**Evidence:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1395`][1]: `| MCI reset | Root, as the reset lock holder |` — The event table names Root as the owner after MCI reset, but qualifies it as the "reset lock holder".
- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1444-1445`][2]: `| 1 | Requester/lock holder |` — Encoding 1 is labelled "Requester/lock holder", so a lock holder owner can legitimately read back as 1. This supports the test's expectation.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_csrs_warm_rst/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_csrs_warm_rst.c:113-114`][3]: `if(mcu_mbox_read_sram_owner(mbox_num) != MCU_MBOX_SRAM_OWNER_REQUESTER) {` — The test expects encoding 1 and its message says "reset lock holder", so its author read "lock holder" as the encoding-1 owner.

**Verified:** The spec's event table says the owner after MCI reset is "Root, as the reset lock holder". The SRAM_OWNER encoding table separates 1 (Requester/lock holder) from 2 (Root). The test expects 1. A spec change that states the exact SRAM_OWNER value after reset would resolve it.

**Searched:**

- SRAM_OWNER encoding in the spec and library header in `docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md, caliptra_ss_lib.h`: 1 = Requester/lock holder, 2 = Root. The spec does not say which encoding applies after reset.

---

### 1.2 The associated MBOX0_VALID_AXI_USER register is valid only once locked by this bit.

`f_4a7ed506cf39` · vague · severity high · section: src/mci/rtl/mci_reg.rdl > mci_reg > MBOX0_AXI_USER_LOCK

**Gap:** It is not stated what 'valid' means observably for the associated register before it is locked.

**Spec says:**

- [`src/mci/rtl/mci_reg.rdl:831`][4]: `Associated MBOX0_VALID_AXI_USER register is only valid once locked by this bit.`

**Not answered by the spec:** Before MBOX0_AXI_USER_LOCK[i].LOCK is set, is MBOX0_VALID_AXI_USER[i] ignored by the mailbox AXI_USER filter? Is it ignored completely, so a matching AxUSER is rejected unless it matches another locked entry or the default user? And once LOCK is set, is that entry's value used for matching from the next transaction onward?

**Verified:** No spec text says directly that an unlocked MBOX0_VALID_AXI_USER entry is ignored by the filter. The hardware spec defines trusted users and the AXI error for untrusted ones. It never ties "valid" to "locked" in observable terms. It also never says when a newly locked entry takes effect. A reader can infer the intended behavior, but the spec does not state it.

**Searched:**

- VALID_AXI_USER and AXI_USER_LOCK in docs and rdl in `docs/, src/mci/rtl`: Only the vague rdl text, integration strap tables and requirement CSS_Axi_9. None of them define the behavior of an unlocked entry.
- MCU Mailbox Limited Trusted AXI users sections in `HardwareSpecification.md:1295, IntegrationSpecification.md:2291`: They describe the trusted set and the AXI error for untrusted users. They do not say what happens to a matching but unlocked entry, or when a locked entry takes effect.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1295-1299`][5]: It gives the AXI error for untrusted users. Reading an unlocked entry as untrusted is an inference, not stated text. The next-transaction timing is also unspecified.

---

### 1.3 BYTE accesses are not supported by the MCU mailbox SRAM.

`f_07a2b69adf09` · missing detail · severity medium · section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Fully addressable SRAM

**Gap:** The behavior when a BYTE access is attempted is not specified.

**Spec says:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1458`][6]: `BYTE accesses are not supported by the SRAM.`

**Not answered by the spec:** For a sub-DWORD access to the MCU mailbox SRAM (AxSIZE<2 or partial WSTRB), what AXI response is returned, and is any SRAM data modified?

**Verified:** I found no spec text that says what the MCU mailbox SRAM returns or does on a sub-DWORD access. The only sub-DWORD rules in the docs apply to the USB AXI interfaces or to the MCU SRAM. The mailbox sentence only says BYTE accesses are unsupported.

**Searched:**

- byte access, WSTRB, partial write, unaligned, SLVERR, DWORD-only in `docs/*.md`: Only USB-specific rules, the MCU SRAM alignment statement (undefined behavior), and the access-control SLVERR rule. None covers the MCU mailbox SRAM.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1981-1983`][7]: The DWORD-only section applies to the USB AXI interfaces, not the MCU mailbox.
- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1975`][8]: The SLVERR behavior is for AXI USER filter denials, not for sub-DWORD accesses.

---

### 1.4 Accesses to the MCU mailbox SRAM must be DWORD aligned.

`f_67fe7c20865e` · missing detail · severity medium · section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Fully addressable SRAM

**Gap:** The behavior on a misaligned access (error response, ignore, etc.) is not specified.

**Spec says:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1458`][6]: `Accesses must be DWORD aligned`

**Not answered by the spec:** What happens on a non-DWORD-aligned AXI access to the MCU mailbox SRAM? Specifically: what response code is returned, is a write committed (and to which address), and what data does a read return?

**Verified:** No spec text defines what happens on a misaligned access to the MCU mailbox SRAM. The related passages I found are about other blocks or say "undefined".

**Searched:**

- misalign, unaligned, DWORD aligned in `docs/`: Hits are for the USB streaming boot spec, the MCU SRAM (undefined behavior), the MCI CSR side-effect note (MCU read fault) and the USB AXI restrictions. None covers the MCU mailbox SRAM response.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1885`][9]: It covers the MCU SRAM, not the mailbox SRAM, and it only says the behavior is undefined.
- [`docs/usb2_streaming_boot_spec/README.md:419`][10]: It describes coercion of unaligned addresses in the USB block, not in the mailbox.
- [`docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md:1025`][11]: It covers MCI CSR accesses made by the MCU, not the mailbox SRAM.

### 1.5 CMD_STATUS is unused by MCU when MCU holds the lock.

`f_4ed0758b1ae5` · vague · severity medium · section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox MCU Access

**Gap:** 'Unused' does not say whether MCU writes are ignored, blocked, or whether reads return a defined value.

**Spec says:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1534`][12]: `` `CMD_STATUS` | Unused ``

**Not answered by the spec:** When MCU holds the lock, is an MCU write to CMD_STATUS blocked (register unchanged) or accepted (value stored but ignored by the protocol)? What value does a read return in that state?

**Verified:** No other spec text says what happens to an MCU write to CMD_STATUS, or what a read returns, when MCU holds the lock. The other passages only repeat that CMD_STATUS is "not used" in that case.

**Further evidence found by the verifier:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1436-1437`][13] — Repeats the same vague wording. It says nothing about write blocking or the read value.
  ~~~
  `CMD_STATUS` is not used when MCU is the Requester, because there is no
  external Requester to serve.
  ~~~

**Searched:**

- cmd_status, case-insensitive, in md/rdl/rst/txt files in `whole repo`: Hits are the hardware spec (lines 1365-1534), the integration spec sequence diagrams (2362-2416) and mcu_mbox_csr.rdl. None defines write or read behavior when MCU holds the lock.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl:140`][14]: The field description says 'Not used when MCU is itself the Requester', which is the same vague wording.

---

### 1.6 Each bit corresponds to locking the associated MBO0X_VALID_AXI_USER register.

`f_46eb300913e4` · unclear referent · severity medium · section: src/mci/rtl/mci_reg.rdl > mci_reg > MBOX1_AXI_USER_LOCK

**Gap:** The register name MBO0X_VALID_AXI_USER appears to be a typo, and 'each bit' does not match the single-bit LOCK field.

**Spec says:**

- [`src/mci/rtl/mci_reg.rdl:847`][15]: `Each bit corresponds to locking the associated MBO0X_VALID_AXI_USER register.`

**Not answered by the spec:** Does MBOX1_AXI_USER_LOCK[i] lock (make read-only) and validate MBOX1_VALID_AXI_USER[i] for the same index i, with no effect on any MBOX0_VALID_AXI_USER register?

**Verified:** No other spec text settles the question. The MBOX1 lock description is a copy of the MBOX0 one and still names MBOX0_VALID_AXI_USER. The integration spec only refers to `MBOX*_VALID_AXI_USER` and `MBOX*_AXI_USER_LOCK` generically. Nothing states that index i of one lock register maps to index i of the matching VALID_AXI_USER array.

**Searched:**

- AXI_USER_LOCK / VALID_AXI_USER in all markdown and rdl files in `repo-wide`: Only mci_reg.rdl and the integration spec (generic wildcard mention). The integration spec points to a hardware spec anchor, but the hardware spec has no matching section or text.
- VALID_AXI_USER and AXI_USER_LOCK in CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md in `docs/`: No matches

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md:2291-2298`][16]: It says the registers must be configured, but gives no index mapping and does not say whether MBOX0 and MBOX1 are independent.

### 1.7 Hardware resets TARGET_STATUS to BUSY on each Target handoff.

`f_3f562f5bcad1` · missing detail · severity medium · section: src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl > mcu_mbox_csr > mbox_target_status

**Gap:** The target handoff event is not defined, and BUSY is not an enum name (the enum uses CMD_BUSY).

**Spec says:**

- [`src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl:106`][17]: `Reset to BUSY by hardware on each target handoff.`
- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1434`][18]: ``Hardware resets `TARGET_STATUS` to BUSY on each Target handoff.``

**Not answered by the spec:** Which exact event performs the hardware clear of TARGET_STATUS: Root setting TARGET_USER_VALID, Root writing TARGET_USER, or another transition? Is the cleared value CMD_BUSY (4'd0), and in which cycle relative to that event does the clear occur?

**Verified:** No specification text names the event or cycle for the hardware clear of TARGET_STATUS. The hardware spec and the RDL both only say "on each Target handoff". Nearby sections describe the TARGET_USER, TARGET_USER_VALID and ownership transitions, but none of them mentions the TARGET_STATUS reset. The RTL clears on mailbox release or on target grant, which the spec never states.

**Searched:**

- TARGET_STATUS, handoff and target_status in md and rdl files in `repository`: The hardware spec, the integration spec and the RDL repeat or only use TARGET_STATUS. None defines when the hardware reset happens.
- Hardware spec lines 1320-1445 read in context in `docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md`: The target grant and ownership sections describe the TARGET_USER and TARGET_USER_VALID clears. They never say that the grant (TARGET_USER_VALID set) resets TARGET_STATUS.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1349-1353`][19]: This describes the re-grant flow and the clears of TARGET_USER and TARGET_USER_VALID. It says nothing about the TARGET_STATUS reset.

## 2. Spec contradicts itself

### 2.1 Integration spec MCU-to-target flow orders EXECUTE and TARGET_USER_VALID opposite to the HW spec

`f_4ca1667d89be` · found during verification · severity medium

**Gap:** The Integration Specification diagram writes TARGET_USER_VALID=1 and then EXECUTE=1. The Hardware Specification ownership table says EXECUTE=1 with TARGET_USER_VALID=0 leaves Root as owner. It also says Root writing TARGET_USER_VALID=1 while EXECUTE=1 hands ownership to the target. The test follows the Hardware Specification.

**Evidence:**

- [`docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md:2327-2328`][20] — The integration diagram has EXECUTE after the grant, which conflicts with the HW spec.
  ~~~
  MCU->>MBOX: Write TARGET_USER_VALID=1
  MCU->>MBOX: Write EXECUTE=1
  ~~~
- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1398`][21]: ``| Root writes `TARGET_USER_VALID=1` while `EXECUTE=1` | The agent named by `TARGET_USER` |`` — HW spec makes the TARGET_USER_VALID write the handoff, with EXECUTE already 1. This is the test's order.
- [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1397`][22]: ``| `EXECUTE=1` while `TARGET_USER_VALID=0` | Root |`` — Setting EXECUTE before the grant is a defined state: Root keeps ownership. The test comment says the same.

**Verified:** The Integration Specification diagram writes TARGET_USER_VALID=1 and then EXECUTE=1. The Hardware Specification ownership table says EXECUTE=1 with TARGET_USER_VALID=0 leaves Root as owner. It also says Root writing TARGET_USER_VALID=1 while EXECUTE=1 hands ownership to the target. The test follows the Hardware Specification.

**Searched:**

- TARGET_USER_VALID and EXECUTE ordering in the HW spec in `docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md`: Ownership table and surrounding text make the TARGET_USER_VALID=1 write, while EXECUTE=1, the handoff to the target.
- Test flow around the grant in `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user.c lines 170-230`: EXECUTE is set first, then TARGET_USER and TARGET_USER_VALID are written, then the test waits for TARGET_STATUS.

---

## 3. Test contradicts its own description

### 3.1 smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data: comment vs. code

`f_1583e4389781` · comment vs. code · severity high

**Gap:** The comment says the lock request made while MCU holds the lock will be checked on the MCU side to confirm the interrupt is set. The Caliptra side only issues the lock request (s12). None of the MCU-side checks (k12-k16) reads or checks a SOC request interrupt status. Plan item p6.k1 (SOC request interrupt is set) is therefore never checked.

**Test description says:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:365`][23]: `// Attempt to acquire lock even though MCU has the lock (will be checked on MCU side that interrupt is set)`
- [`docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx [Subsystem Test Plan] row 121`][24]: `SOC request interrupt set` — SOC request interrupt is set.

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:367`][25]: `caliptra_ss_mcu_mbox_acquire_lock(mbox_num, 1);` — stimulus; Request the mailbox lock while MCU holds it.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:115`][26]: `if(!mcu_mbox_wait_for_user_lock(mbox_num, caliptra_uc_axi_id, 10000)) {` — check; Caliptra must acquire the mailbox lock with the expected AXI user within the wait limit; otherwise a fatal message is printed, exit status 1 is sent and the test hangs.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:121`][27]: `if(!mcu_mbox_wait_for_user_execute(mbox_num, 1, 10000)) {` — check; Caliptra must set execute within the wait limit.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:64`][28]: `if (mbox_resp_data != clptra_expected_data[ii]) {` — check; Each SRAM dword written by Caliptra must equal the expected data.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:130`][29]: `if(lsu_read_32(SOC_MCI_TOP_MCU_MBOX0_CSR_MBOX_USER + MCU_MBOX_NUM_STRIDE * mbox_num) != caliptra_uc_axi_id) {` — check; The MBOX_USER CSR must equal the Caliptra AXI user ID that locked the mailbox.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:164`][30]: `if(!mcu_mbox_wait_for_user_execute(mbox_num, 0, 10000)) {` — check; Caliptra must clear execute after reading the response data within the wait limit.

**Verified:** The Caliptra-side comment says the MCU side will check that the SOC-request-while-MCU-lock interrupt is set. The MCU-side pass_data test never reads or checks that interrupt. It does not call the library helper that waits for it, and it has no ISR or interrupt references. Other tests (smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user) do use that helper, but this one does not.

**Searched:**

- interrupt/intr/SOC_REQ in the pass_data MCU test in `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/*.c`: no interrupt check in the MCU-side file; only a comment and unused variables in the Caliptra file
- soc_req_while_mcu_lock helper usages in `src/integration`: used only in smoke_test_mcu_hitless and smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user

---

### 3.2 mci_mbox0_ownership_flow: plan vs. test

`f_481566381db1` · plan vs. test · severity medium

**Gap:** The plan item says the MCU acquires the mailbox lock, passes data to a SoC agent, and reads the new data after the DATA_READY response. In the test, the MCU is only the root, which sets trusted users and releases the mailbox. A random SoC user (the requester) acquires the lock, and another SoC user is the target. The roles in the plan are reversed relative to what the test drives.

**Test description says:**

- [`docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx`][24]: `MCU acquires lock, passes data to SOC agent, which responds DATA_READY; MCU reads new data.`
- [`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:178`][31]: `// Requester and Target are random trusted SoC users; Root is the MCU.`

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:179`][32]: `mcu_mbox_pick_soc_users(soc_users, 2);` — stimulus; Pick two random SoC users as requester and target; root is the MCU AXI user.
- [`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:180`][33]: `mci_comm_flow_requester = soc_users[0];` — stimulus; Pick two random SoC users as requester and target; root is the MCU AXI user.
- [`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:181`][34]: `mci_comm_flow_root = cptra_ss_strap_mcu_lsu_axi_user_i;` — stimulus; Pick two random SoC users as requester and target; root is the MCU AXI user.
- [`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:182`][35]: `mci_comm_flow_target = soc_users[1];` — stimulus; Pick two random SoC users as requester and target; root is the MCU AXI user.
- [`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:195`][36]: `mcu_mbox_set_trusted_users(mbox_num, soc_users);` — stimulus; Set trusted users, release the mailbox as root, and have the requester acquire the lock.
- [`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:196`][37]: `mcu_mbox_release(mbox_num, mci_comm_flow_root);` — stimulus; Set trusted users, release the mailbox as root, and have the requester acquire the lock.
- [`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:197`][38]: `mcu_mbox_acquire_lock(mbox_num, mci_comm_flow_requester);` — stimulus; Set trusted users, release the mailbox as root, and have the requester acquire the lock.

**Verified:** The plan rows say the MCU gets the lock and passes data to a SoC agent. In the test the MCU is only the root, and a random SoC user is the requester that acquires the lock. The roles are reversed. Nothing in the repository ties the plan rows to this test by name, so the link is inferred. I found no other test that realizes the plan rows.

**Searched:**

- Plan rows about the MCU getting the lock and passing data to a SoC agent in `docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx`: Rows B137 and B138 match the finding's quote. No test name is given in those rows.
- Other tests realizing the plan rows (DATA_READY, mcu_mbox) in `src, docs`: Only mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh uses DATA_READY in the testbench. mcu_mbox_soc_agent_write_fw_image.svh is the only other mailbox test I checked; it uses COMPLETE.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mcu_mbox_soc_agent_write_fw_image.svh:16-20`][39]: It is a SoC agent firmware-image write flow that uses COMPLETE. It does not realize the DATA_READY plan row.

### 3.3 mci_error_non_fatal_warm_rst_check: check cannot fail

`f_6f160b38b849` · check cannot fail · severity medium

**Gap:** The message calls this an 'SVA ERROR' and says HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL is expected to remain unchanged on warm reset. The assertion's failure action only prints that message, so a violation does not fail the test. The stability requirement is therefore reported but never enforced.

**Test description says:**

- [`src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv:245`][40]: `SVA ERROR: MCI HW ERROR NON FATAL is expected to remain unchanged on warm reset`

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv:244`][41]: ``((~`CPTRA_SS_TOP_PATH.cptra_ss_rst_b_i & `CPTRA_SS_TOP_PATH.cptra_ss_pwrgood_i) |-> ($stable(`MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mci_reg_hwif_out.HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL)[*5]))`` — check; does not fail the test; When cptra_ss_rst_b_i is low and power good is high, HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL is stable for 5 cycles. Disabled when power good is low. A violation only prints a message.

**Verified:** The assertion's else branch is only `$display("SVA ERROR: ...")`. It does not call `$error` or `$fatal`, and it sets no failure flag. Nothing in the repository that I found scans logs for "SVA ERROR" to fail a test. A violation is therefore printed but does not fail the test. I did not check whether the simulator's own assertion-failure count feeds into pass/fail.

**Searched:**

- uses of the assertion name in `whole repo`: Only the definition at line 241; no test references it.
- a log or checker that fails the test on 'SVA ERROR' in `whole repo, excluding the SVA file itself`: Only other $display-based SVA files; no consumer of the string.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/integration/testbench/caliptra_ss_top_tb_services.sv:1`][42]: It contains $error/$fatal-style matches, but none that parse SVA messages.

---

### 3.4 mci_error_non_fatal_cold_rst_check: check cannot fail

`f_71de8c2f726c` · check cannot fail · severity medium

**Gap:** The assertion is presented as a check: the message reads 'SVA ERROR: MCI HW ERROR NON FATAL is expected to reset on cold reset'. A violation only prints that message and does not fail the test. A HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL value that survives a cold reset would therefore go unnoticed unless the log is scanned.

**Test description says:**

- [`src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv:239`][43]: `SVA ERROR: MCI HW ERROR NON FATAL is expected to reset on cold reset`

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv:238`][44]: ``(~`CPTRA_SS_TOP_PATH.cptra_ss_pwrgood_i |-> (`MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mci_reg_hwif_out.HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL=='h0))`` — check; does not fail the test; When cptra_ss_pwrgood_i is low, HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL equals 0. A violation only prints a message.

**Verified:** The assertion's else branch only calls $display. No $error or $fatal is used, and nothing outside the .sv files looks for "SVA ERROR" in the logs. A violation therefore prints a message but does not fail the test.

**Searched:**

- "SVA ERROR" in non-.sv files (scripts, makefiles, logs) in `whole repo`: No matches, so no log scanning for this message.
- Other uses of "SVA ERROR" in .sv files in `whole repo`: All are $display-only, matching the same pattern.

### 3.5 all_error_non_fatal_check: check cannot fail

`f_7b20eefcf778` · check cannot fail · severity medium

**Gap:** The assertions k1 and k2 are described as checks, and the message i1 says "SVA ERROR: all_error_non_fatal is asserted unexpectedly". A violation only prints this message and does not fail the test, so a real violation would go unnoticed in the result.

**Test description says:**

- [`src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv:252`][45]: `SVA ERROR: all_error_non_fatal is asserted unexpectedly`

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv:250`][46] — check; does not fail the test; With mbox0/mbox1 ECC masks set, a mailbox double ECC error and mci_intr, all_error_non_fatal stays deasserted for 5 cycles.
  ~~~
  ((`MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mci_reg_hwif_out.internal_hw_error_non_fatal_mask.mask_mbox0_ecc_unc & `MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mci_reg_hwif_out.internal_hw_error_non_fatal_mask.mask_mbox1_ecc_unc) & (`MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mbox0_sram_double_ecc_error | `MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mbox1_sram_double_ecc_error) & `MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mci_intr |=> ~`MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.all_error_non_fatal[*5])
  ~~~
- [`src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv:251`][47]: ``((&`MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mci_reg_hwif_out.internal_fw_error_non_fatal_mask.mask & |`MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mci_reg_hwif_out.FW_ERROR_NON_FATAL.error_code) |=> ~`MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.all_error_non_fatal[*5])`` — check; does not fail the test; With all FW non-fatal mask bits set and a nonzero FW_ERROR_NON_FATAL error_code, all_error_non_fatal stays deasserted for 5 cycles. A violation only prints a message.

**Verified:** all_error_non_fatal_check is an assert property whose only action is `else $display(...)`. It has no $error, $fatal or error counter, so a violation prints a message and does not fail the test. I found no log scanner or testbench hook in the repository that turns "SVA ERROR" into a failure. The simulator's own assertion-failure count may still be flagged outside the repository, which I couldn't check.

**Searched:**

### 3.7 mci_error_non_fatal_check: check cannot fail

`f_b7cfe0f12370` · check cannot fail · severity medium

**Gap:** The summary says a double ECC error must set the matching HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL ECC uncorrectable bit on the next cycle. The message at i1 is labelled "SVA ERROR". The assertion k1 only prints that message on violation, so a failing property does not fail the test and the check cannot fail it.

**Test description says:**

- [`src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv:234`][50]: `SVA ERROR: MCI HW ERROR NON FATAL reg is not set correctly`

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv:233`][51] — check; does not fail the test; mbox0 double ECC error implies HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL.mbox0_ecc_unc next cycle, and mbox1 double ECC error implies mbox1_ecc_unc next cycle. A violation only prints a message.
  ~~~
  (`MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mbox0_sram_double_ecc_error |=> `MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mci_reg_hwif_out.HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL.mbox0_ecc_unc) and (`MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mbox1_sram_double_ecc_error |=> `MCI_REG_TOP_PATH.mci_reg_hwif_out.HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL.mbox1_ecc_unc)
  ~~~

**Verified:** The assertion mci_error_non_fatal_check has only an else $display action. In SystemVerilog, an assert with an else clause runs only that clause on failure, so there is no default error and no $fatal or $error. I found nothing in the repository that scans logs for "SVA ERROR" or otherwise turns this message into a test failure, so the check cannot fail a test. I did not read the regression or pass/fail scripts in full.

**Searched:**

- Log scanners, $assertoff or fatal-on-assert handling that would turn 'SVA ERROR' into a test failure in `whole repository, excluding *sva.sv files`: Nothing that treats 'SVA ERROR' as a failure. The only hits were unrelated $assertoff calls in USB and fuse_ctrl, and -assert compile flags.
- The assertion body and its action block in `src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv lines 215-254`: Every assertion in this file uses an else $display action. None calls $error or $fatal.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`tools/scripts/Makefile:392-401`][52]: These lines only enable assertion compilation. They do not make a failed assertion fail the test.

### 3.8 smoke_test_mcu_mbox: plan vs. test

`f_c45dacf55f05` · plan vs. test · severity medium

**Gap:** The plan says the SOC agent responds with DATA_READY after the MCU acquires the lock and passes data, and the MCU then reads new data. The test only polls for the COMPLETE status (0x2) and then clears execute. It neither waits for DATA_READY nor reads any new data afterward.

**Test description says:**

- [`docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx`][24]: `MCU acquires lock, passes data to SOC agent, which responds DATA_READY; MCU reads new data.`

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox/cptra_ss_mcu_mbox_test.c:233`][53]: `reg = caliptra_ss_mcu_mbox_wait_status_complete(mbox_num, 200);` — stimulus; Poll the command status register up to 200 times for the completion status.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox/cptra_ss_mcu_mbox_test.c:80`][54]: `if (mbox_data == 0x2) {` — check; The command status must reach 0x2 (complete) within 200 polls; otherwise the test reports a timeout and exits with an error status.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox/cptra_ss_mcu_mbox_test.c:234`][55]: `if (reg == 2) {` — check; The command status must reach 0x2 (complete) within 200 polls; otherwise the test reports a timeout and exits with an error status.

**Verified:** The plan says the response status is DATA_READY (1). Both sides of the test use CMD_COMPLETE (2): the SOC side polls for status 2, and the MCU firmware writes 0x2. No DATA_READY wait exists. The finding's claim that no data is read afterward is partly wrong, because the test does read MCU data through caliptra_ss_mcu_mbox_get_data. The status mismatch still stands.

**Searched:**

- DATA_READY usage in the smoke test in `src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox`: No DATA_READY wait. Only status 0x2 is polled and written.
- Status enum encoding in `src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl`: DATA_READY=1, CMD_COMPLETE=2

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox/cptra_ss_mcu_mbox_test.c:242-247`][56]: get_data does read and check data after completion, so only the DATA_READY part of the finding holds.

---

### 3.9 smoke_test_mcu_mbox: check cannot fail

`f_f54d6febfdbe` · check cannot fail · severity medium

**Gap:** The plan item says the user CSR must show the user holding the lock, and the comment says 'Checking MBOX USER INFO'. The code reads the MBOX USER register and only prints it, with no comparison against an expected user, so nothing is checked and the test cannot fail on it.

**Test description says:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox/cptra_ss_mcu_mbox_test.c:112`][57]: `// Checking MBOX USER INFO`
- [`docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx [Subsystem Test Plan] row 117`][24]: `User CSR properly displays the user with lock` — User CSR displays the user holding the lock.

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox/cptra_ss_mcu_mbox_test.c:114`][58]: `soc_ifc_axi_dma_read_ahb_payload(SOC_MCI_TOP_MCU_MBOX0_CSR_MBOX_USER + MCU_MBOX_NUM_STRIDE * mbox_num, 0, read_payload, 4, 0);` — stimulus; Read the MBOX USER register (the value is only printed).

**Verified:** The "Checking MBOX USER INFO" step reads the MBOX USER register and only prints it. Nothing compares the value to an expected user, so the test cannot fail on it. The MCU-side test only polls until USER is nonzero, which is a weak check, and then prints it.

**Further evidence found by the verifier:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox/smoke_test_mcu_mbox.c:115-117`][59]: `while(lsu_read_32(SOC_MCI_TOP_MCU_MBOX0_CSR_MBOX_USER + MCU_MBOX_NUM_STRIDE * mbox_num) == 0);` — The MCU side only waits for USER to be nonzero and does not compare it to an expected user.

**Searched:**

- MBOX_USER uses in the smoke_test_mcu_mbox directory in `src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox`: 3 uses: one read-and-print, one poll until nonzero, and one read into a variable that is printed. No comparison against an expected user.

### 3.10 smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data: plan vs. test

`f_1f10a1ae6b50` · plan vs. test · severity low

**Gap:** Plan item p9 says MCU acquires the lock, passes data to the SOC agent, and the SOC agent responds DATA_READY. In the test the roles are reversed. Caliptra acquires the lock, writes the data and sets execute, and MCU only waits for Caliptra's lock (s25) and returns the response with DATA_READY. MCU never acquires the lock.

**Test description says:**

- [`docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx`][24]: `MCU acquires lock, passes data to SOC agent, which responds DATA_READY; MCU reads new data.`

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:391`][60]: `cptra_mcu_mbox_acquire_lock(mbox_num, 1000, true);` — stimulus; Acquire the mailbox lock.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:115`][26]: `mcu_mbox_wait_for_user_lock(mbox_num, caliptra_uc_axi_id, 10000)` — stimulus; Wait for Caliptra to acquire the mailbox lock.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:157`][61]: `mcu_mbox_write_sram_dword(mbox_num, ii, mbox_data[ii]);` — stimulus; Write the response data to mailbox SRAM, write the data length, and set status to DATA_READY.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:160`][62]: `mcu_mbox_write_dlen(mbox_num, sizeof(mbox_data));` — stimulus; Write the response data to mailbox SRAM, write the data length, and set status to DATA_READY.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:162`][63]: `mcu_mbox_update_status(mbox_num, MCU_MBOX_DATA_READY);` — stimulus; Write the response data to mailbox SRAM, write the data length, and set status to DATA_READY.

**Verified:** Caliptra acquires the lock and MCU only waits for it, then returns the response with DATA_READY. The plan text has MCU acquiring the lock and the SOC agent responding, so the roles are reversed. I did not open the plan spreadsheet; the plan wording comes from the finding.

**Searched:**

- Read both test sources around the lock, execute and response steps in `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data directory`: Caliptra is the lock owner and MCU is the responder.
- Search for pass_data in md files in `repo`: The glob failed in zsh, so nothing was searched. I did not look for another test that realizes the plan item.

### 3.11 smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation: comment vs. code

`f_61330c35c86c` · comment vs. code · severity low

**Gap:** The intent text contradicts itself about the Caliptra uC's AXI user. i2 and i8 say the uC is configured as a valid AXI user, but i4, i5 and the error messages for k1-k11 say 'invalid user'. The code sets the mailbox valid user to a random axi_user_id and configures the DMA user as caliptra_uc_axi_id, so the description of the scenario is inconsistent. The errors are expected because the mailbox is not instantiated, not because of an invalid user, so the messages describe a different condition than the one under test. This also makes the link to plan item p1 (invalid-user rejection) unclear.

**Test description says:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:36`][64]: `// 1. MCU will configure Caliptra uC to be a valid AXI`
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:41`][65]: `MCU will configure Caliptra uC to be an invalid AXI`
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:60`][66]: `Caliptra: CSR Writes and Read as Valid AXI but no Mbox instantiation`
- [`docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx [Subsystem Test Plan] row 104`][24]: `Invalid user AXI accesses are rejected and report AXI error` — Invalid user AXI accesses are rejected and report AXI error

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:60`][67]: `mcu_cptra_init_d(.cfg_cptra_dma_axi_user=true, .cptra_dma_axi_user=caliptra_uc_axi_id, .cfg_mcu_mbox0_valid_user=mbox0_sel, .mcu_mbox0_valid_user=axi_user_id, .cfg_mcu_mbox1_valid_user=!mbox0_sel, .mcu_mbox1_valid_user=axi_user_id);` — stimulus; Initialize MCU and Caliptra, configuring the Caliptra DMA AXI user and the MCU mailbox valid users.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:64`][68]: `Caliptra: Expected AXI Error writing to CSR EXECUTE as invalid user` — check; AXI write and read of CSR EXECUTE must return an AXI error (status false); otherwise fatal print, error control code and hang.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:71`][69]: `Caliptra: Expected AXI Error reading CSR EXECUTE as invalid user` — check; AXI write and read of CSR EXECUTE must return an AXI error (status false); otherwise fatal print, error control code and hang.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:65`][70]: `SEND_STDOUT_CTRL(0x1);` — check; AXI write and read of CSR EXECUTE must return an AXI error (status false); otherwise fatal print, error control code and hang.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:208`][71]: `Caliptra: Expected AXI Error writing to Mbox%x SRAM as invalid user` — check; Each of the random AXI writes and reads to the mailbox SRAM must return an AXI error.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:217`][72]: `Caliptra: Expected AXI Error reading Mbox%x SRAM as invalid user` — check; Each of the random AXI writes and reads to the mailbox SRAM must return an AXI error.
- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:63`][73]: `if(!status) {` — check; Each of the random AXI writes and reads to the mailbox SRAM must return an AXI error.

**Verified:** The prose is inconsistent. The MCU-side test says the Caliptra uC is a valid AXI user. The Caliptra-side test header and its error messages say invalid user, and one of its log lines says "Valid AXI but no Mbox instantiation". The code sets the DMA user to caliptra_uc_axi_id and the mailbox valid users to axi_user_id. The AXI errors it expects come from the mailbox not being instantiated, not from user rejection.

**Searched:**

- valid/invalid wording in both test sources in `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation directory`: The two sources contradict each other. The messages say invalid user even though the test targets a mailbox that is not instantiated.

### 3.12 smoke_test_mcu_mbox0_write_user_lock: check cannot fail

`f_79e630809b5c` · check cannot fail · severity low

**Gap:** The Caliptra-side comment says "Checking MBOX USER INFO", but the code only reads the USER register over AXI DMA into read_payload. No extracted check compares the value with the expected user, so this step cannot fail the test.

**Test description says:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock.c:66`][74]: `// Checking MBOX USER INFO`

**Test does:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock.c:67`][75]: `soc_ifc_axi_dma_read_ahb_payload(SOC_MCI_TOP_MCU_MBOX0_CSR_MBOX_USER + MCU_MBOX_NUM_STRIDE * mbox_num, 0, read_payload, 4, 0);` — stimulus; Read the mailbox USER register.

**Verified:** The Caliptra-side code reads the MBOX USER register and only prints it with VPRINTF. Nothing compares it with an expected value, so this step cannot fail the test. The MCU-side file only checks that a write to USER leaves it unchanged. That is a separate check and does not validate the Caliptra-side value.

**Searched:**

- USER references in the test directory in `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock/`: Caliptra side only reads and prints. MCU side only does a read-only write check.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock.c:102-107`][76]: This checks that the USER register is read-only. It does not compare the USER value Caliptra read against an expected user.

## 5. Spec not tested

### 5.1 Partly tested: 4 requirements in CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > MCU Mailbox Limited Trusted AXI users (and 1 more section)

`f_469ba16e55c7` · severity high

1. **Requirement** `cr_7f11ce310155`: There are 5 trusted AXI users, determined at build time via parameters or via MCI lockable registers.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Limited Trusted AXI users

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1297`][77]: `There are 5 trusted AXI Users determined at build time via parameters or via MCI lockable registers.`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_axi_param`:** Exercises the Caliptra uC AXI ID set either from a build-time parameter or from the CSR user IDs, and checks AXI errors depending on which is used.
   **Not tested:** Does not verify that there are exactly 5 trusted users, or that each parameter or CSR user slot is accepted as trusted.

   **Verified:** No test checks every one of the 5 slots or rejects a sixth ID.

3. **Requirement** `cr_bb5623ddc32e`: Only the trusted AXI users, the default user 0xFFFF_FFFF, and MCU can access or obtain a lock on the mailbox.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Limited Trusted AXI users

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1297`][77]: `These users, a default user 0xFFFF_FFFF, and MCU are the only AXI users that can access or obtain a lock on the mailbox.`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_invalid_axi`:** A user outside the allowed set gets AXI errors on all CSR and SRAM accesses.
   **Not tested:** Does not check lock acquisition by such a user, nor that the default user 0xFFFF_FFFF or MCU are allowed.

   **Verified:** No test checks lock denial for an untrusted user, or access by the default user.

4. **Requirement** `cr_045d5c38ebd5`: If a second Target user is required, MCU waits for the first Target to publish a terminal TARGET_STATUS.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Target User

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1349-1350`][78]: ``If a second Target user is required, MCU shall wait for the first Target to publish a terminal `TARGET_STATUS` during normal operation.``

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user`:** MCU waits for the first Target to publish a COMPLETE terminal TARGET_STATUS before the mailbox flow continues.
   **Not tested:** No second Target is required or programmed after the first one finishes, so the wait-then-re-grant sequence is not exercised.

   **Verified:** The target_user test has a single Target only.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user.c:211`][79]: `// Wait for the Target to publish a terminal TARGET_STATUS` — The test has a single Target and no second Target is granted.

5. **Requirement** `cr_261014384edd`: Hardware clears and unlocks both registers when the mailbox is released.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Target User

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1328-1329`][80]: ``Hardware clears and unlocks both registers on a terminal `TARGET_STATUS` write or when the mailbox is released.``

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mbox_csr_zeros_after_release`:** Clearing EXECUTE releases the mailbox directly, and the test then checks that TARGET_USER and TARGET_USER_VALID read 0.
   **Not tested:** It does not check that the registers are unlocked after release, for example by writing them again.

   **Verified:** The release test only reads the registers back as 0 and never rewrites them.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mbox_csr_zeros_after_release/smoke_test_mbox_csr_zeros_after_release.c:103`][81]: `lsu_write_32(SOC_MCI_TOP_MCU_MBOX0_CSR_MBOX_EXECUTE + MCU_MBOX_NUM_STRIDE * mbox_num, 0);` — The test releases the mailbox and only reads the registers back as 0. It never rewrites them to show they are unlocked.

Item 2 of this group was not verified (see Withdrawn and Inconclusive).

**Searched:**

- Item 1: tests for 5 trusted slots and a sixth ID in `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_axi_param and smoke_test_mcu_mbox_invalid_axi`: axi_param reads MBOX_LOCK once. invalid_axi uses 5 random IDs and then an ID outside them. No test exercises each slot or checks for a sixth.
- Item 2: trusted user lock then CSR writes in `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data`: Caliptra acquires the lock and writes CMD, DLEN and EXECUTE. The MCU reads DLEN back. CMD_STATUS and USER are not covered.
- Item 3: untrusted user acquiring the lock, and the default 0xFFFFFFFF or MCU user in `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_invalid_axi`: MBOX_LOCK is in the list of accesses an invalid user attempts (line 88). No check that the lock stays free, and no positive check for the default user.
- Item 4: second Target user in `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user`: Only a single Target flow.
- Item 5: rewrite after release in `smoke_test_mbox_csr_zeros_after_release`: Only reads the registers back as 0 after release.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data.c:103-120`][82]: This is a positive trusted-user flow after acquiring the lock. It does not read back all the CSRs the gap names.

### 5.2 Partly tested: 1 requirement in CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > MCU Mailbox MCU Access

`f_6ae2ab31ed12` · severity high

1. **Requirement** `cr_2d3206a4c6cc`: TARGET_USER and TARGET_USER_VALID are writable only while TARGET_USER_VALID=0.

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1516-1517`][83]
     ~~~
     Both registers are writable only while
     `TARGET_USER_VALID=0`.
     ~~~

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_strb_wr_csr`:** TARGET_USER is written byte by byte while VALID=0 and takes effect. After VALID is set, a write of 0 to TARGET_USER is ignored.
   **Not tested:** A write to TARGET_USER_VALID while it is 1 is not attempted.

   **Verified:** No test writes TARGET_USER_VALID while it is 1.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock.c:146`][84]: `Wasn't able to write Mbox%x TARGET_USER_VALID as Root` — Only checks a successful write of TARGET_USER_VALID, not a blocked one.

Items 2, 3, 4, 5 of this group were not verified (see Withdrawn and Inconclusive).

**Searched:**

- TARGET_USER_VALID in tests in `src/integration/test_suites`: Only writes while 0, checks for changed-by-target, and clear checks; no blocked write while 1.

### 5.3 Partly tested: 5 requirements in CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > MCU Mailbox Status Registers (and 2 more sections)

`f_60dc44ecdea3` · severity high

1. **Requirement** `cr_62191b6deb64`: Only the current Target writes TARGET_STATUS (the mailbox target status register).
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Status Registers

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1429-1430`][85]
     ~~~
     Only the current Target
     writes `TARGET_STATUS`
     ~~~
   - [`src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl:106`][17]: `Written only by the current target user and read by Root.`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user`:** The target's writes are ignored before ownership is granted, and after the grant the target publishes a COMPLETE target status that MCU observes.
   **Not tested:** Writes by other non-target agents to TARGET_STATUS are not checked.

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_strb_wr_csr`:** An 8-bit write of TARGET_STATUS from Root is attempted and the test checks the value is unchanged.
   **Not tested:** Only a Root write is checked. The Target's write succeeding and other agents being blocked are not checked.

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox0_write_user_lock`:** Root attempts to write TARGET_STATUS and the test checks it is unchanged.
   **Not tested:** Only a Root write is blocked. The current Target's write succeeding and other non-target users being blocked are not checked.

   **Verified:** No non-target, non-Root write-block check found; the existing tests cover only Root and the target.

2. **Requirement** `cr_ccc86edf401d`: When DLEN = 0 the entire MBOX SRAM is cleared.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox SRAM Clearing

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1473`][86]: `**NOTE**: When DLEN = 0 the entire MBOX SRAM is cleared.`

   **Tested by `mcu_mbox_rand_rdwr`:** The final step writes DLEN equal to the full SRAM size and checks that the max address reads 0. This is not DLEN = 0.
   **Not tested:** Never sets DLEN to 0, so the whole-SRAM clear for DLEN = 0 is not verified.

   **Verified:** No DLEN=0 whole-SRAM clear test found.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_mbox_lock_return_one_during_zeroize/mcu_mbox_lock_return_one_during_zeroize.c:39`][87]: `const uint32_t mbox_dlen = 10000;` — This test uses a large non-zero DLEN and checks only that the lock reads 1 during zeroize. It does not cover DLEN=0 or the clearing phases.

3. **Requirement** `cr_fadd412c3094`: Clearing flow order: requester releases lock, SRAM clearing starts, SRAM clearing ends, then mailbox is unlocked.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox SRAM Clearing

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1462`][88]: `The flow for clearing the SRAM is:`
   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1464-1467`][89]: `1. Requester releases lock 2. MCU SRAM starts clearing 3. MCU SRAM clearing ends 4. Mailbox is unlocked`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_zeroize`:** Releases the lock and then re-acquires it, and checks that the SRAM is zero at that point. This indirectly shows that the unlock follows the clearing.
   **Not tested:** Does not observe the intermediate phases of the sequence. Only the end state after unlock is checked.

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_usr_lock_out_zero`:** Shows that the lock cannot be acquired during zeroization after the release. This is consistent with the order: release, then clearing, then unlock.
   **Not tested:** Does not check that clearing starts and ends in order or that the unlock happens only after the clearing ends.

   **Verified:** Only the end state and 'lock reads 1 during zeroize' are checked. No assertion or test observes clearing start and end.

4. **Requirement** `cr_20362a1989f1`: On a single bit ECC error while reading the mailbox, the data is corrected.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Errors

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1504`][90]: `Single Bit ECC Error`
   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1504`][90]: `Data corrected`
   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1504`][90]: `Single bit ECC error while reading Mailbox.`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_ecc`:** Injects SB ECC errors and reads back the written addresses, but only the interrupt and status are checked.
   **Not tested:** No check that the data read back after the single-bit error equals the written (corrected) data.

   **Verified:** The data read after a single-bit error injection is not compared.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_ecc/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_ecc.c:102`][91]: `rand_data = mcu_mbox_read_sram_dword(mbox_num, rand_dword_addr);` — Read-back data is assigned but never compared to the written or expected data. Only the interrupt and status are checked.

5. **Requirement** `cr_feb29b2f4853`: On a double bit ECC error while reading the mailbox, invalid data is returned.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Errors

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1505`][92]: `Double Bit ECC Error`
   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1505`][92]: `Invalid data returned`
   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1505`][92]: `Double bit ECC error while reading Mailbox.`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_ecc`:** Reads back addresses after DB ECC injection and checks the interrupt and status, but not the returned data.
   **Not tested:** No check of the data returned on the read that hits the double-bit error.

   **Verified:** The data returned on a double-bit error read is not checked.

**Searched:**

- TARGET_STATUS writes by non-target agents in `src/integration/test_suites (12 files reference it)`: The cited tests only cover Root and the target. The invalid_axi test only lists the register among CSRs it exercises with invalid AXI accesses. No non-target SoC user or Requester write-block check was found.
- DLEN=0 followed by an SRAM clear check in `src/integration/test_suites`: No test writes DLEN=0 and then reads back the whole SRAM.
- Clearing-phase ordering checks (start, end, unlock) via assertions in `src/integration/asserts/caliptra_ss_top_sva.sv and RTL files that mention zeroize/clearing`: No mcu_mbox clearing assertions in the SVA file. Only functional lock-reads-1 tests exist.
- Single-bit and double-bit ECC read-data checks in `src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_ecc`: No data comparison. Only interrupt, status and HW_ERROR_NON_FATAL are checked.
- Corrected/invalid data checks for ECC in other tests in `src/integration/test_suites`: Nothing found beyond the ECC smoke test.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mbox_csr_zeros_after_release/smoke_test_mbox_csr_zeros_after_release.c:107-111`][93]: It checks the DLEN register reads 0 after release. It does not write DLEN=0 or check that the SRAM is cleared.

### 5.4 Partly tested: 4 requirements in CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > MCU Mailbox Target User (and 1 more section)

`f_e38d580bc493` · severity high

1. **Requirement** `cr_29fbbfde9d79`: Hardware clears and unlocks both registers on a terminal TARGET_STATUS write.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Target User

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1328-1329`][80]: ``Hardware clears and unlocks both registers on a terminal `TARGET_STATUS` write``

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user`:** After the Target publishes a COMPLETE terminal status, the test checks that TARGET_USER reads 0 and TARGET_USER_VALID reads 0.
   **Not tested:** It does not check that the registers are unlocked after the terminal write, for example by writing them again.

   **Verified:** I found no test that rewrites the registers after a terminal TARGET_STATUS. I did not read the other test directories.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_write_user_lock.c:144-149`][94] — Root sets valid; no later attempt to clear TARGET_USER_VALID or rewrite TARGET_USER afterwards.
     ~~~
     mcu_mbox_write_target_user_valid(mbox_num, 1);
         if (mcu_mbox_read_target_user_valid(mbox_num) != 1) {
     ~~~

2. **Requirement** `cr_4c05e98bd6a2`: Writing TARGET_USER_VALID=1 locks both target configuration registers against all software writes.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Target User

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1326-1327`][95]: ``Writing `TARGET_USER_VALID=1` locks both registers against all software writes.``

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_strb_wr_csr`:** After TARGET_USER_VALID is set, a write of 0 to TARGET_USER is attempted and the test checks that it is ignored.
   **Not tested:** Writes to TARGET_USER_VALID after the lock are not attempted, and only a single TARGET_USER write is tried.

   **Verified:** I found no test that makes repeated writes to both registers after the lock. I did not read the other test directories.

3. **Requirement** `cr_8e68a65f2d45`: After the grant, software cannot retarget the mailbox or clear TARGET_USER_VALID.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Target User

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1327-1328`][96]: `Software cannot retarget the mailbox or clear valid after the grant.`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_strb_wr_csr`:** After valid is set, a write to TARGET_USER is attempted and the test checks that the value is unchanged, so retargeting is blocked.
   **Not tested:** No attempt to clear TARGET_USER_VALID after the grant is made or checked.

   **Verified:** I found no test where Root writes 0 to TARGET_USER_VALID after the grant. I did not read the other test directories.

4. **Requirement** `cr_afbf3aa32a43`: Root (typically MCU) is the only agent that may write/program TARGET_USER and TARGET_USER_VALID.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md > Manufacturer Control Interface (MCI) > MCI Feature Descriptions > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Target User

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1318-1319`][97]: ``Root, typically MCU, is the only agent that programs `TARGET_USER` and `TARGET_USER_VALID`.``
   - [`docs/CaliptraSSHardwareSpecification.md:1515-1516`][98]
     ~~~
     Root is the only agent that may write `TARGET_USER` and
     `TARGET_USER_VALID`.
     ~~~

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user`:** The Target attempts writes to CSRs before it is made valid, and TARGET_USER is still 0 before the grant. This shows the Target cannot program TARGET_USER.
   **Not tested:** The test does not directly check that a non-Root agent cannot write TARGET_USER_VALID. Other non-Root agents are not tried.

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_valid_user`:** A non-Root user attempts to write TARGET_USER and TARGET_USER_VALID. The test checks that TARGET_USER does not read back 0xdeadbeef and that TARGET_USER_VALID stays 0.
   **Not tested:** Only one kind of non-Root agent (the requester) is tried. The Target user, SoC agents and other trusted users are not tried, and the writes are not tried while valid is 0 versus 1.

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation`:** An untrusted (not-valid) user writes and reads TARGET_USER and TARGET_USER_VALID and each access must return an AXI error.
   **Not tested:** Only an untrusted user is tried, and only on an uninstantiated mailbox. Trusted non-Root agents such as the requester or Target are not tried.

   **Verified:** I did not find tests that try writes from the Target or from other trusted non-Root agents with valid at both 0 and 1. I did not read the other test directories.

Item 5 of this group was not verified (see Withdrawn and Inconclusive).

**Searched:**

- TARGET_USER_VALID usage in C tests in `src/integration/test_suites`: 11 files; I read only write_user_lock in detail. It does not test the lock, relock or clear-after-grant behaviour.
- rewrite after terminal TARGET_STATUS (cr_29fbbfde9d79) in `write_user_lock test`: No rewrite of TARGET_USER or TARGET_USER_VALID after a terminal status.
- writes after lock (cr_4c05e98bd6a2) in `write_user_lock test`: Only the initial valid=1 write; no later writes to either register.
- clear after grant (cr_8e68a65f2d45) in `write_user_lock test`: No write of 0 to TARGET_USER_VALID after the grant.
- non-Root writers (cr_afbf3aa32a43) in `write_user_lock test`: Only Root writes the target registers; no Target or other-agent write attempts.

### 5.5 Partly tested: 5 requirements in CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md > MCU MBOX SRAM Sizing Requirements (and 3 more sections)

`f_a55fc0460027` · severity high

1. **Requirement** `cr_318abb731c64`: The MCU can be configured to implement up to 2 independent mailboxes.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md > MCI > MCI Integration Requirements > MCU MBOX SRAM Sizing Requirements

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md:2263`][99]: `The MCU can be configured to implement up to 2 independent mailboxes`

   **Tested by `mci_mbox_dlen_wrap`:** The test runs its SRAM staging and wipe procedure on both MCU mailboxes, deriving each mailbox's SRAM size from HW_CONFIG0. This shows that two mailboxes are present and working.
   **Not tested:** It does not check independence between the two mailboxes or that a mailbox configured with size 0 is not instantiated. It also does not check that there are no more than 2.

   **Verified:** An instantiation test exists, but independence and the limit of 2 mailboxes are unchecked.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_instantiation.c:60`][66]: `VPRINTF(LOW, "Caliptra: CSR Writes and Read as Valid AXI but no Mbox instantiation\n");` — This covers the not-instantiated mailbox case, which is part of item 1. It does not cover independence or the limit of 2 mailboxes.

2. **Requirement** `cr_554ae7995021`: If build-time straps are not used, trusted MBOX AXI users must be configured via the MBOX*_AXI_USER_LOCK register before SOC agents can use the MBOX.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md > MCI > Programming interface > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Limited Trusted AXI users

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md:2293`][100]: `If build-time integration straps are not used for configuring the trusted MBOX AXI users, then trusted users will need to configured with the following lockable MCI registers before MBOX can be used by SOC agents:`
   - [`docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md:2296`][101]: `` `MBOX*_AXI_USER_LOCK` ``

   **Tested by `mcu_mbox_soc_agent_write_fw_image`:** The SoC agent polls until the MBOX AXI user lock reads 1, then writes the FW image, DLEN, CMD and EXECUTE into the mailbox. The AXI write responses must be OKAY and the command status must reach complete. This shows the mailbox is usable once the lock is set.
   **Not tested:** Does not check that SoC agent accesses are rejected before the users are configured and locked. It also does not exercise the case where build-time straps are absent.

   **Verified:** No test references AXI_USER_LOCK, and none checks rejection before locking.

3. **Requirement** `cr_daf7f972a12c`: If build-time straps are not used, trusted MBOX AXI users must be configured via the MBOX*_VALID_AXI_USER register before SOC agents can use the MBOX.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md > MCI > Programming interface > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Limited Trusted AXI users

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md:2293`][100]: `If build-time integration straps are not used for configuring the trusted MBOX AXI users, then trusted users will need to configured with the following lockable MCI registers before MBOX can be used by SOC agents:`
   - [`docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md:2295`][102]: `` `MBOX*_VALID_AXI_USER` ``

   **Tested by `mcu_mbox_soc_agent_write_fw_image`:** The SoC agent polls until the MBOX valid AXI user register equals its own AXI user. It then uses the mailbox with accepted AXI writes, and the command status must reach complete. This shows the mailbox is usable after the valid user is configured.
   **Not tested:** Does not check that accesses are rejected before the valid user is programmed. It does not exercise the no-strap case, and the poll on the valid-user register has no check of its own.

   **Verified:** No test references VALID_AXI_USER, and none checks rejection before the register is programmed.

4. **Requirement** `cr_3f38fb82f957`: When MBOX_DLEN is 0 and MBOX_EXECUTION is cleared, the clearing logic erases the entire MBOX SRAM.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md > MCI > Programming interface > MCU Mailbox > MCU Mailbox Doorbell Command DLEN

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md:2306`][103]: `When MBOX_DLEN = 0 and MBOX_EXECUTION is cleared, the clearing logic erases the entire MBOX SRAM.`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_zeroize`:** The test sets and clears execute after writing data, then re-acquires the lock and checks that the SRAM is zero up to the max DLEN. This exercises the clear-on-execute-clear path, but only with a nonzero DLEN.
   **Not tested:** The DLEN = 0 case is never exercised. The test does not check that the entire MBOX SRAM is erased when DLEN is 0 and execute is cleared, because it only checks zeroing up to mbox_dlen.

   **Verified:** The only DLEN=0 execute-clear test checks the lock value, not that the SRAM is zero.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_mbox_lock_return_one_during_zeroize/mcu_mbox_lock_return_one_during_zeroize.c:69`][104]: `VPRINTF(LOW, "MCU: Mbox%x write DLEN: 0x0\n", mbox_num, mbox_dlen);` — This is the only test that clears EXECUTE with DLEN=0. It checks only that lock reads 1 during zeroize, not that the SRAM is zero.

5. **Requirement** `cr_de12a01e738b`: After EXECUTE=1 is written, the mailbox owner becomes SOC.
   Section: docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md > MCI > Programming interface > MCU Mailbox > MCU to SOC Target Flow

   - [`docs/CaliptraSSIntegrationSpecification.md:2329`][105]: `Note over MBOX,SOC: owner=SOC`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user`:** After the grant the target's accesses to the mailbox succeed, which shows target ownership indirectly. Nothing checks the SRAM owner field or HW_STATUS owner directly.
   **Not tested:** Direct check that the owner reads SOC/Target after EXECUTE=1 with a valid target

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox0_write_user_lock`:** After TARGET_USER_VALID is written the SRAM owner is checked to be Target and the Target stays owner. The valid bit is written after the requester's EXECUTE, not as an MCU-staged request followed by EXECUTE=1.
   **Not tested:** The MCU-requester ordering of TARGET_USER_VALID then EXECUTE=1 causing the owner change

   **Tested by `mci_comm_flow_transaction`:** Walks a transaction in which Root grants the Target after EXECUTE=1. At the target-owns-request phase it checks that the expected SRAM owner is the Target and that non-owner accesses are blocked.
   **Not tested:** Requester here is not the MCU, and the owner is checked at a phase level rather than immediately after the EXECUTE write

   **Verified:** I found no direct owner check immediately after EXECUTE=1 in an MCU-requester flow. This rests on a grep, not a detailed read of every target-user test.

**Searched:**

- DLEN=0 followed by execute clear in the mbox tests in `src/integration/test_suites/*mbox*/*.c`: Only mcu_mbox_lock_return_one_during_zeroize, with no SRAM-zero check.
- VALID_AXI_USER or AXI_USER_LOCK references in `src/integration mbox tests`: No matches.
- Mailbox independence, HW_CONFIG0 and MCU_MBOX1 usage in `src/integration/test_suites/*mbox*/*.c`: The instantiation test covers the no-mailbox case. Nothing checks cross-mailbox independence.
- Owner check right after EXECUTE=1 with TARGET_USER_VALID in `src/integration/test_suites/*mbox*/*.c`: The target_user and write_user_lock tests check the owner, but not in an MCU-requester ordering immediately after EXECUTE=1.
- Rejection before AXI users are locked in `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_invalid_axi and smoke_test_mcu_mbox_valid_user`: The invalid-AXI test uses a user that is invalid by configuration. It does not test the unconfigured state before locking.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_invalid_axi/cptra_smoke_test_mcu_mbox_invalid_axi.c:45`][106]: It checks that an invalid user gets AXI errors. It does not cover the before-lock or no-strap programming flow.

### 5.6 Partly tested: 3 requirements in mcu_mbox_csr.rdl > mbox_target_user_valid (and 2 more sections)

`f_66e7dd7d0b36` · severity high

1. **Requirement** `cr_8c87d704146c`: Software writes to TARGET_USER_VALID are accepted only from Root while the field is 0.
   Section: src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl > mcu_mbox_csr > mbox_target_user_valid

   - [`src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl:79`][107]: `Writable only by Root while this field is 0.`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox0_write_user_lock`:** Root writes TARGET_USER_VALID while it is 0 and it reads back 1, so Root acceptance is verified.
   **Not tested:** Rejection of non-Root writes and of writes when the field is already 1 is not checked.

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_valid_user`:** A non-Root user's write of 1 to TARGET_USER_VALID is checked to be rejected (field stays 0).
   **Not tested:** Root acceptance while the field is 0 is not checked, nor is rejection of Root writes once the field is 1.

   **Verified:** No check found that Root's re-write of TARGET_USER_VALID is ignored once the field is 1, or that a non-Root write is rejected while it is 0 (the smoke tests only cover part of this).

3. **Requirement** `cr_7aa648529b38`: mcu_mbox_csr.mbox_target_status.status: software writes take effect only when mcu_mbox_csr.valid_target_req is asserted.
   Section: src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl > mcu_mbox_csr > mbox_target_status

   - [`src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl:122`][108]: `swwe=valid_target_req`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_target_user`:** The target's SRAM and CSR accesses are attempted while TARGET_USER_VALID is 0 and nothing changes. After the grant, the target's COMPLETE status write takes effect.
   **Not tested:** A TARGET_STATUS write specifically attempted while valid_target_req is deasserted is not clearly checked as blocked. The target-not-valid CSR check reads back zeros, which could be the reset value.

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_strb_wr_csr`:** A Root byte write of 0x3 to TARGET_STATUS is attempted and the register is checked to be unchanged.
   **Not tested:** The write comes from Root, not a target user, so the case of a valid target with valid_target_req asserted is not covered.

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox0_write_user_lock`:** A Root write to TARGET_STATUS is attempted and TARGET_STATUS is checked to be unchanged.
   **Not tested:** The write comes from Root with no valid target. Writes by a valid target are not checked.

   **Verified:** No test attempts a TARGET_STATUS write by the future target while valid_target_req is low, and none checks that the write is rejected again after the field is cleared.

5. **Requirement** `cr_b84a00bfdcae`: Only the MCU writes the status field.
   Section: src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl > mcu_mbox_csr > mbox_cmd_status

   - [`src/mci/rtl/mcu_mbox_csr.rdl:130`][109]: `Written only by MCU and polled by the Requester.`

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_valid_user`:** A non-MCU user (the Caliptra uC requester) attempts to write MCU_MBOX_CMD_FAILURE to CMD_STATUS, and the test checks it still reads 0.
   **Not tested:** Only one non-MCU agent is exercised; Target and other SoC users are not tried.

   **Tested by `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_pass_data`:** The non-MCU Caliptra requester attempts to write CMD_STATUS and the test checks the written value is not read back.
   **Not tested:** Only one non-MCU agent is exercised; Target and other SoC users are not tried.

   **Verified:** No test has the Target or a SoC agent write CMD_STATUS while it is not MCU-owned and then checks the value is unchanged. Only the Caliptra uC requester is exercised.

Items 2, 4 of this group were not verified (see Withdrawn and Inconclusive).

**Searched:**

- Root re-writes of TARGET_USER_VALID, and non-Root writes to it, in tests and the SV testbench in `src/integration`: The only Root write is the grant in the ownership flow. There is no re-write after the field reads 1, and no check that a Root write is accepted again after hardware clears it.
- Terminal TARGET_STATUS values and the resulting TARGET_USER_VALID clear and ownership change in `src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh`: All three terminal statuses are run. The test checks sram_owner returns to Root. It does not read TARGET_USER_VALID directly.
- TARGET_STATUS writes attempted while valid_target_req is low in `src/integration`: The ownership flow's non-owner writes are SRAM and EXECUTE only. The smoke tests write TARGET_STATUS only as Root. Nothing tests a future target's write being blocked.
- TARGET_DONE notification for each terminal status in `src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_common_tasks.svh`: mcu_mbox_wait_clear_notif fatals if TARGET_DONE never sets, and the test runs it for every terminal status. The test does not check that non-terminal values leave the notification unset.
- Target or SoC writes to CMD_STATUS while it is not MCU-owned in `src/integration`: None. Only the Caliptra uC requester attempt in the smoke tests, which is already partial.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:77-87`][110]: Non-owner agents write only SRAM and EXECUTE here. They do not write TARGET_STATUS, TARGET_USER_VALID or CMD_STATUS, so this does not cover items 1, 3 or 5.

### 5.8 Test plan items with no test: 4 plan items

`f_a06f18895dc1` · severity medium

1. **Plan item:** MCU MBOX - on reset deassertion MCU automically gets lock — On reset deassertion the MCU automatically acquires the mailbox lock. (`docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx`)

   **Verified:** No test checks that MCU holds the lock after reset deassertion. The helper only clears it.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/libs/caliptra_ss_lib/caliptra_ss_lib.c:314-323`][114]: `void mcu_mbox_clear_lock_out_of_reset(uint32_t mbox_num) {` — The reset-lock helper only clears DLEN and EXECUTE and prints a message. It does not check that MCU holds the lock after reset.

2. **Plan item:** MCU MBOX - Get lock on mailbox by one user and verify the USER value with an agent with a different USER ID. — One agent acquires the MCU mailbox lock, then the USER value is verified from an agent with a different USER ID. (`docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx`)

   **Verified:** The USER value is read only by MCU itself. No second agent with a different USER ID checks it.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_axi_param/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_axi_param.c:47`][115]: `uint32_t axi_select = xorshift32() % 5;` — The randomization is of the Caliptra uC AXI user selection. It is not MCU LSU AXI USER randomization.

3. **Plan item:** MCU MBOX - SRAM size between 0 and 2 MB in 1KB sizes (0, 81, 512, 2MB) — Exercise the MCU mailbox with SRAM sizes from 0 to 2 MB in 1KB granularity. (`docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx`)

   **Verified:** The tests use whatever SRAM size is configured. I found no sweep over the listed sizes.
   - [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_valid_user/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_valid_user.c:126-130`][116]: `uint32_t sram_size_kb = mcu_mbox_get_sram_size_kb(mbox_num);` — The tests read the configured SRAM size. They do not sweep the sizes 0, 81, 512 and 2MB.

5. **Plan item:** MCU MBOX - randomize MCU LSU AXI USER — Randomize the MCU LSU AXI USER value on mailbox accesses. (`docs/Caliptra_Gen2_SS_TestPlan.xlsx`)

   **Verified:** MCU_LSU_AXI_USER is not randomized on mailbox accesses. The only randomization found is the Caliptra uC AXI selection.

Item 4 of this group was not verified (see Withdrawn and Inconclusive).

**Searched:**

- tests that check the MCU lock after reset deassertion in `test_suites mcu_mbox*`: Only the mcu_mbox_clear_lock_out_of_reset helper, which clears the lock without checking it.
- SRAM size sweep and parameter configurations in `test_suites yml files and C files`: The tests only read the configured size. No sweep over sizes was found.
- randomized MCU LSU AXI USER and cross-agent USER check in `test_suites`: target_user compares MBOX_USER to MCU_LSU_AXI_USER, but the value is not randomized.
- valid user CSR works with LOCK set and the parameter not enabled in `valid_user and axi_param tests`: Close but not confirmed. valid_user has MCU acquire the lock and use CSR valid users. axi_param checks CSR-configured users.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_valid_user/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_valid_user.c:96-110`][117]: MCU acquires the lock and checks that the USER is MCU. This is not verified from a different-USER agent, and it does not check the lock held at reset.

### 5.51 Register access: no suite covers every register

`f_e684b2415bb9` · register suite · severity low

**Gap:** 81 register properties of this kind are not covered by a test that runs over every register: the suites found cover only some of the registers.

**Suites that touch these registers:** `smoke_test_mcu_mbox_strb_wr_csr`

**Verified:** I found no generic register test that runs over the MCU mailbox CSR block. mci_reg_rand_test sweeps only the MCI-register-space mailbox valid-user registers (REG_GROUP_MCI_MBOX0/1). Its group list leaves out REG_GROUP_MCU_MBOX0, which holds the mcu_mbox CSRs (CMD, DLEN, EXECUTE, TARGET_STATUS and so on). The cited strobe-write test checks only a subset of registers directly.

**Further evidence found by the verifier:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_strb_wr_csr/smoke_test_mcu_mbox_strb_wr_csr.c:93`][259]: `if(mcu_mbox_read_cmd(mbox_num) != mask) {` — The suite directly checks only a handful of registers (DLEN, CMD, CMD_STATUS, TARGET_STATUS, TARGET_USER). It is not a full per-register sweep.

**Searched:**

- UVM register sequences (reg_hw_reset, bit_bash, reg_access) in `whole repo`: none
- generic register tests in `src/integration/test_suites`: mci_reg_rand_test, which does not include the REG_GROUP_MCU_MBOX0 group. smoke_test_mcu_mbox_csrs_warm_rst is a separate directed test.

**Looked at and ruled out:**

- [`src/integration/test_suites/mci_reg_rand_test/mci_reg_rand_test.c:46-57`][260]: It touches mailbox registers, but only the MCI-space AXI-user registers. It does not touch the MCU_MBOX CSR block.
