## Inconclusive

The verifier could neither confirm nor refute these. They need a human decision.

- `f_469ba16e55c7` (partly tested) Trusted users require the MBOX lock to write the mailbox CSRs. — pass_data has a positive lock-then-write flow, but CMD_STATUS and USER are not exercised and the readback is weak.
- `f_6ae2ab31ed12` (partly tested) When an external Requester holds the lock, MCU is the only agent that may write CMD_STATUS. — Not searched in depth.
- `f_6ae2ab31ed12` (partly tested) EXECUTE is RW for MCU while MCU owns the SRAM, whether MCU or an external Requester holds the lock. — Not searched in depth.
- `f_6ae2ab31ed12` (partly tested) MCU write access to mailbox CSRs is determined by its role in the current transaction and follows the status register writer rules. — Not searched in depth.
- `f_6ae2ab31ed12` (partly tested) LOCK and USER access for MCU follows mailbox locking rules in both lock cases. — Not searched in depth.
- `f_e38d580bc493` (partly tested) Ownership conditions are evaluated in the stated precedence order. — I did not examine the ownership-flow tests or the RTL precedence logic, so I could not confirm or refute the precedence-combination gap.
- `f_a06f18895dc1` (test plan items with no test) MCU MBOX - Valid user CSR works when LOCK set and parameter not enabled — valid_user and axi_param touch this area but I could not confirm that either covers the exact condition.
- `f_fa3367a3301d` (partly tested) The SOC requester writes SRAM, DLEN and CMD to stage the command. — I did not search the staging-flow tests for a CMD or DLEN readback.
- `f_87f1e04df40b` (no test found) The event counter increments on the rising edge of the hardware interrupt event input. — The software-trigger count check indirectly exercises the increment for cmd_avail, but not a hardware input edge.
- `f_a40e976ebc6e` (no test found) The incrementor signal pulses for 1 clock cycle from the same input signal that sets the sticky interrupt event. — The 1-cycle pulse is only constrained indirectly by the summed count comparison, and the test drives the trigger rather than the hardware event input.
- `f_8e312cf1f5e1` (no test found) mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox1_cmd_avail_intr_count_incr_r.pulse: the field is a counter. — The field being a counter is an implementation property. It is only implied by the count registers matching, with no direct check.
- `f_8e312cf1f5e1` (no test found) The pulse is a 1-bit down-counter that decrements immediately on being set, producing a pulse. — The 1-bit down-counter pulse mechanism is internal. No test checks it directly.
- `f_c879f7a38c02` (no test found) mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox0_ecc_cor_intr_count_incr_r.pulse: the field is a counter. — Only implicitly exercised by the count-sum check; there is no direct test.
- `f_c879f7a38c02` (no test found) mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_cptra_mbox_cmd_avail_intr_count_incr_r.pulse: the field is a counter. — Only implicitly exercised by the count-sum check; there is no direct test.
- `f_c879f7a38c02` (no test found) mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox0_ecc_cor_intr_count_incr_r.pulse: the counter decrements by 1. — The decrement is only implicitly exercised by the count-sum check; there is no direct test.
- `f_bb99ac035c0a` (no test found) The event counter increments by 1 for every interrupt event. — `mcu_intr_handler` checks that firmware and hardware counts match after one trigger per event, so +1 per event is partly checked. It does this through the software trigger path and in aggregate. The trigger-to-increment path in the RTL was only partly read.

Withdrawn items from the groups in section 5 (37):

- Writing any terminal value clears TARGET_USER_VALID. — The ownership flow runs DATA_READY, COMPLETE and CMD_FAILURE as terminal Target writes, then checks that SRAM ownership returns to Root. ([`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:202-204`][287])
- Writing any terminal value notifies Root. — Every terminal status in the ownership flow waits for TARGET_DONE, which fatals if it is missing. ([`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:202-204`][287])
- MCU MBOX - Verify DEFAULT_USER (1s for all USER bits) is considered a Valid User even when not explicitly set by VALID USER parameter or CSR — smoke_test_mcu_mbox uses 0xFFFFFFFF as Caliptra's user with no valid user configured. ([`src/integration/test_suites/smoke_test_mcu_mbox/smoke_test_mcu_mbox.c:164-165`][288])
- Each interrupt has enable, status, trigger, and count register fields in the MCI register block with _en, _sts, _trig, and _intr_count_r suffixes. — mcu_intr_handler triggers each MBOX interrupt and checks the hardware count registers against its own count. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:201-204`][203])
- SRAM_OWNER value 0 means no owner. — The ownership flow checks SRAM_OWNER == NONE after release. ([`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:94-95`][289])
- Reads of the MCU mailbox SRAM are not destructive. — The owner's repeated reads must return the same data in every phase, which checks that reads are non-destructive. ([`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:83-84`][290])
- No software owner when MBOX_LOCK is not set. — The ownership flow checks that the SRAM owner is NONE in the released phase, where the lock is free, and stimulus drives EXECUTE=0 to reach it. ([`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:94-95`][289])
- The MCU mailbox sends the notif_mbox*_cmd_avail interrupt to the MCU when a command is available. — The ownership-flow test sets EXECUTE and then waits on the CMD_AVAIL notification status. ([`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_common_tasks.svh:489-491`][291])
- The pulse is a 1-bit down-counter that decrements immediately after being set, producing a pulse. — A pulse longer than 1 cycle would make the counts mismatch in mcu_intr_handler. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256-258`][292])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox1_ecc_cor_intr_count_incr_r.pulse: the counter decrements by 1. — decrvalue=1 is implicitly checked by the exact event count in mcu_intr_handler. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256-258`][292])
- The event counter increments by 1 for every interrupt event. — mcu_intr_handler triggers one event each and checks the counter sums match, including NOTIF_MBOX0_ECC_COR. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256-258`][292])
- The event counter increments by 1 for every interrupt event. — mcu_intr_handler triggers every notif and error interrupt and fails if the hardware counter sum differs from the firmware per-event count. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256-257`][225])
- The event counter increments by 1 for every interrupt event. — The mcu_intr_handler test fires one trigger per event, including CPTRA_MBOX_CMD_AVAIL and MBOX1_CMD_AVAIL, and fails if the summed hardware counts differ from the firmware count. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256-259`][208])
- The event counter increments by 1 for every interrupt event. — mcu_intr_handler triggers the mbox1_ecc_cor notification once and checks that the summed counters match the number of events. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256-259`][208])
- The event counter increments by 1 for every interrupt event. — mcu_intr_handler triggers each event and the summed counts must equal the firmware count, so it checks increment by 1 per event. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:213-216`][293])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox1_ecc_cor_intr_count_r.cnt: the counter increments by 1. — mbox1_ecc_cor is triggered once, and the summed hardware counters must equal the fw count. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:215-221`][294])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_cptra_mbox_cmd_avail_intr_count_r.cnt: the field is a counter. — The cptra_mbox_cmd_avail counter is triggered (line 111) and read back (line 215), and the sum is compared. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:111`][295])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox0_cmd_avail_intr_count_r.cnt: the field is a counter. — The mbox0_cmd_avail counter is triggered and read back, and the sum is compared. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:215-221`][294])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox1_soc_req_lock_intr_count_r.cnt: the counter increments by 1. — mbox1_soc_req_lock is triggered once (line 117), and the summed counters must equal the fw count. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:117`][296])
- The event counter increments by 1 for every interrupt event. — mcu_intr_handler.c triggers soc_req_lock events and checks the summed counters equal the software count. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256`][297])
- The event counter increments by 1 for every interrupt event. — mbox1 target_done is triggered and its counter is included in the summed check. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256`][297])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox1_cmd_avail_intr_count_r.cnt: the counter increments by 1. — mcu_intr_handler triggers MBOX1 CMD_AVAIL once and checks the HW counter sum against the FW count. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:201-204`][203])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.error_mbox0_ecc_unc_intr_count_r.cnt: the counter increments by 1. — MBOX0 ECC_UNC is triggered once and its counter is in the sum that is checked. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:201-204`][203])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.error_mbox1_ecc_unc_intr_count_r.cnt: the field is a counter. — MBOX1 ECC_UNC is triggered and its counter is checked in the sum, which shows the field counts. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:201-204`][203])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox0_target_done_intr_count_r.cnt: the counter increments by 1. — MBOX0 TARGET_DONE is triggered once and its counter is in the checked notif sum. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:211-214`][298])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox1_soc_req_lock_intr_count_r.cnt: the field is a counter. — mcu_intr_handler triggers MBOX1_SOC_REQ_LOCK once and checks that the summed counters match the SW count. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256-259`][208])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox0_cmd_avail_intr_count_r.cnt: the counter increments by 1. — The test triggers MBOX0_CMD_AVAIL once; the summed-count check would fail if the increment were not 1. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256-259`][208])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.notif_mbox0_ecc_cor_intr_count_r.cnt: the field is a counter. — The test triggers MBOX0_ECC_COR once and the summed counters are checked. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:256-259`][208])
- mci_reg.intr_block_rf.error_mbox1_ecc_unc_intr_count_r.cnt: the counter increments by 1. — The test triggers MBOX1_ECC_UNC once; the summed-count check would fail if the increment were not 1. ([`src/integration/test_suites/mcu_intr_handler/mcu_intr_handler.c:201-204`][203])
- sram_owner value NONE (0) means no software agent owns the SRAM. — mci_mbox_ownership_flow checks sram_owner == NONE after release, and the check errors on mismatch. ([`src/integration/testbench/sv_tests/mci/mci_mbox_ownership_flow.svh:93-95`][299])
